0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}},\zeta}({\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\mathcal{V}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\mathcal{V}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{V}\hfil$\crcr}}}},\mathcal{V}))
by
Lemma
20
.
∎

We are left with proving the converse property, namely that

{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathsf{op}}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
for each closed type
\sigma
. This
requires a bit more work, and is based on a notion of “finite” approximation
of elements of the model, that we define by syntactic means as follows.

4.4.1.
Restriction operators

We define
14
14

14

This definition as well as our
reasoning below features some similarities with
step-indexing
that we
would like to understand better.

closed terms
{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)
and
{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)
(for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\sigma
a
type and
\varphi
a positive type) typed as follows:

\vdash{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma):{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\sigma}
and

\vdash{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi):{\varphi}\multimap{\varphi}
.

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\oc{\varphi}}\,\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)\rangle\mathsf{der}\,x

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi}\multimap{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\lambda f^{\oc({\varphi}\multimap{\sigma})}\,\lambda x^{\varphi}\,\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)\rangle(\langle\mathsf{der}\,f\rangle\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)\rangle x)^{\mathord{\oc}}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\mathbf{1})

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\mathbf{1}}\,x

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\oc\sigma)

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\oc\sigma}\,(\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)\rangle x)^{\mathord{\oc}}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{{\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}}}\,\left(\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{\ell})\rangle\mathsf{pr}_{\ell}x,\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{r})\rangle\mathsf{pr}_{r}x\right)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{{\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}}}\,\mathsf{case}(x,x_{\ell}\cdot\mathsf{in}_{\ell}\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{\ell})\rangle x_{\ell},x_{r}\cdot\mathsf{in}_{r}\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{r})\rangle x_{r})

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(0,\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi)

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi}\,\Omega^{\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n+1,\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi)

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi}\,\mathsf{fold}(\langle{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi}\left[{\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])\rangle\mathsf{unfold}(x))

This is a well-founded lexicographic inductive definition on triples

(n,\sigma,l)
(where
l\in\{\mathsf{v},\mathsf{g}\}
) if we order the symbols

\mathsf{v}
and
\mathsf{g}
by
\mathsf{v}<\mathsf{g}
. We consider actually only
triples
(n,\sigma,l)
such that
\sigma
is positive if
l=\mathsf{v}
.

We describe similarly the interpretation of these terms: we give an
explicit description of the matrices
\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{}
and

\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}
. To this end, we define a family of sets

{\mathsf{I}}^{l}(n,\sigma)\subseteq|{[{\sigma}]}|

by induction on
(n,\sigma,l)
(where
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

l\in\{\mathsf{v},\mathsf{g}\}
and
\sigma
is a closed type which is
positive if
l=\mathsf{v}
).

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\oc{\sigma})=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}({{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)})

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})={\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{\ell})\times{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{r})

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})=\{\ell\}\times{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{\ell})\cup\{r\}\times{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi_{r})

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(0,\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi)=\emptyset

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n+1,\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi)={\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\varphi}\left[{\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi}/{\zeta}\right])

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi)={\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)

•

{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi}\multimap{\sigma})={\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)\times{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\varphi
be a closed positive type and
\sigma
be a closed
type. One has

\displaystyle\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}_{(a,b)}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }a=b\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}\quad\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{}_{(c,b)}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }c=[{b}]\text{ and }b\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

By Theorem
16
, for a closed positive type
\varphi
and for

u\in\mathsf{P}^{\mathord{\oc}}{[{\varphi}]^{\oc}}
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right](u)_{a}=\begin{cases}u_{a}&\text{if }a\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

And for a closed type
\sigma
and for
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
, it suffices
to prove

\displaystyle\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right](u^{!})_{a}=\begin{cases}u_{a}&\text{if }a\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Both statements are easily proved by induction.
∎

We need now to prove that, given
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
, the sequence

\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{(u^{!})}
is monotone and has
u
as lub.

Lemma 33
.

For any triple
(n,\sigma,l)
where
\sigma
is positive if

l=\mathsf{v}
, one has

{\mathsf{I}}^{l}(n,\sigma)\subseteq{\mathsf{I}}^{l}(n+1,\sigma)
. Moreover

\displaystyle\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}{\mathsf{I}}^{l}(n,\sigma)=|{[{\sigma}]}|\,.

Proof.

The first statement is straightforward, by induction on
(n,\sigma,l)
.
For the second statement, we only have to prove the right-to-left
inclusions. We define the
height

\mathsf{h}(a)
of an element

a
of
|{[{\sigma}]}|

as follows.

•

\mathsf{h}(*)=1

•

\mathsf{h}(a_{1},a_{2})=1+\max{(\mathsf{h}(a_{1}),\mathsf{h}(a_{2}))}
(this definition
is used when
\varphi
is a tensor and when
\sigma
is a linear implication)

•

\mathsf{h}(i,a)=1+\mathsf{h}(a)

•

\mathsf{h}([{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}}])=1+\max{(\mathsf{h}(a_{1}),\dots,\mathsf{h}(a_{k}))}

Then by induction on
\mathsf{h}(a)
one proves that

\displaystyle\forall a\in|{[{\sigma}]}|\,\exists n\in{\mathbb{N}}\ a\in{\mathsf{I}}^{l}(n,\sigma)

We deal only with the statement relative to
{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)
. The
closed positive type
\varphi
is of shape

\displaystyle\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{1}\cdot\cdots\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{k}\cdot\psi

where
\psi
is not of shape
\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\rho
. We introduce auxiliary closed
types
\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{k}
as follows:

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}

\displaystyle=\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{1}\cdot\cdots\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{k}\cdot\psi

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}

\displaystyle=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{2}\cdot\cdots\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta_{k}\cdot{\psi}\left[{\varphi_{1}}/{\zeta_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \vdots

\displaystyle\varphi_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\psi}\left[{\varphi_{1}/\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2}/\zeta_{2},\dots,\varphi_{k}/\zeta_{k}}\right]

and all these types have the same interpretation in
\mathbf{Pcoh}^{\mathord{\oc}}
. The type

\psi
cannot be one of the type variables
\zeta_{i}
as otherwise we would
have
|{[{\varphi}]}|=\emptyset
, contradicting our assumption that
a

belongs to this set. Assume that
\psi=\oc\sigma
so that we must have

a=[{b_{1},\dots,b_{l}}]
with
b_{i}\in|{[{\sigma^{\prime}}]}|
(where

\sigma^{\prime}={\sigma}\left[{\varphi_{1}/\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2}/\zeta_{2},\dots,\varphi_{k}/\zeta_{k}}\right]
)
for each
i=1,\dots,l
. We have
\mathsf{h}(b_{i})<\mathsf{h}(a)
for each
i
so that
we can apply the inductive hypothesis: for each
i
there is
n_{i}
such that

b_{i}\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n_{i},\sigma^{\prime})
. Using the monotonicity property (first
statement of the lemma) and setting
n=\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{l})
we have

b_{i}\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma^{\prime})
and hence
a\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\oc{\sigma^{\prime}})
. Therefore

a\in{\mathsf{I}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n+k,\varphi)
(coming
back to the definition of this set). The other
cases are dealt with similarly.
∎

Lemma 34
.

Let
\sigma
be a closed type and let
\varphi
be a closed positive
type. If
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
then the sequence

({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is monotone
(in
\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
) and has
u
as lub. If

u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
then the sequence

({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{u})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is monotone and has

u
as lub.

Proof.

Immediate consequence of Lemmas
32

and
33
.
∎

4.4.2.
Main Inclusion Lemma

Now we are in position of proving the key lemma in the proof of the uniqueness
of relations.

Lemma 35
.

Let
\sigma
be a closed type,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

l\in\{\mathsf{v},\mathsf{g}\}
. If
l=\mathsf{g}
and

(M,u)\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}
then

(M,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}})\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}
.
If
l=\mathsf{v}
and
\sigma
is a closed positive type
\varphi
then

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{-}}\Rightarrow(V,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v})\in{(\overline{\mathcal{V}(\varphi)})}^{\mathord{+}}={\mathcal{R}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{+}}
.

Proof.

By lexicographic induction on triples
(n,\sigma,l)
(with
\sigma

positive when
l=\mathsf{v}
).

Until further notice, we assume that
l=\mathsf{v}
.

The only case where “
n
decreases” in this induction is when

\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi
, we start with this case.

Assume that
\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi
and that

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{-}}
. If
n=0
we have

{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v}=0
and hence

(V,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{+}}
by
Lemma
30
. Assume that the implication
holds for
n
and let us prove it for
n+1
. Let

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{-}}
, that is
V=\mathsf{fold}(W)
with

(W,v)\in{\mathcal{V}({\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}^{\mathord{-}}
. We have

{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n+1,\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi)}\right]{}}\,{v}={\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v}

by definition. By inductive hypothesis we have

\displaystyle(W,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v})\in{\mathcal{R}({\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}^{\mathord{+}}

(117)

and we must prove that

(\mathsf{fold}(W),{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{+}}
.
Let
(T,t)\in{({\mathcal{V}(\varphi)}\multimap{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})})}^{\mathord{-}}
, we must
prove that

(\langle T\rangle\mathsf{fold}(W),t({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v}))\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})
.
Let
S=\lambda x^{{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right]}\,\langle T\rangle\mathsf{fold}(x)
, we have

(S,t)\in{({\mathcal{V}({\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\multimap{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})})}^{\mathord{-}}

by Lemma
21
and therefore

(\langle S\rangle W,t({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v}))\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})

by (
117
) and Lemma
22
and this
implies
(\langle T\rangle\mathsf{fold}(W),{t}\,{({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right])}\right]{}}\,{v})})\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})
by
Lemma
21
.

Assume that
\varphi=\oc\sigma
and that

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}(\oc\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}
, that is
V=M^{\mathord{\oc}}
and

v=u^{!}
with
(M,u)\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}
. By inductive
hypothesis we have

(M,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]}\,{u^{!}})\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}

and hence

(M^{\mathord{\oc}},({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]}\,{u^{!}})^{!})\in{\mathcal{V}(\oc\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}

and since

({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]}\,{u^{!}})^{!}={\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\oc\sigma)}\right]}\,{u^{!}}
we get

(V,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\oc\sigma)}\right]}\,{v})\in{\mathcal{V}(\oc\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}\subseteq{\mathcal{R}(\oc\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}

as expected.

Assume that
\varphi={\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}}
and that

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}({\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{-}}
, that is

V=\left(V_{\ell},V_{r}\right)
and
v={v_{\ell}}\otimes{v_{r}}
with

(V_{i},v_{i})\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi_{i})}^{\mathord{-}}
for
i\in\{\ell,r\}
. By
inductive hypothesis we have

(V_{i},{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi_{i})}\right]}\,{v_{i}})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi_{i})}^{\mathord{+}}
. By
Lemma
23
we get

(\left(V_{\ell},V_{r}\right),{{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi_{\ell})}\right]}\,{v_{\ell}}}\otimes{{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi_{r})}\right]}\,{v_{r}}})\in{\mathcal{R}({\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{+}}
,
that is

(\left(V_{\ell},V_{r}\right),\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})}\right]{}({v_{\ell}}\otimes{v_{r}}))\in{\mathcal{R}({\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{+}}
.

Assume that
\varphi={\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}}
and

(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}({\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{-}}
. This means that
for some
i\in\{\ell,r\}
, one has
V=\mathsf{in}_{i}W
and

v={{\mathrm{in}}_{i}}\,{w}
for
(W,w)\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi_{i})}^{\mathord{-}}
. By
inductive hypothesis we have

(W,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi_{i})}\right]{}}\,{w})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi_{i})}^{\mathord{+}}

and hence

(\mathsf{in}_{i}W,{{\mathrm{in}}_{i}}\,{({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi_{i})}\right]{}}\,{w})})\in{\mathcal{R}({\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{+}}

by Lemma
25
, that is

(\mathsf{in}_{i}W,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})}\right]{}}\,{w})\in{\mathcal{R}({\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}})}^{\mathord{+}}
.

We assume now that
l=\mathsf{g}
.

If
\sigma
is a closed positive type
\varphi
and let

(M,u)\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}
, we must prove that

(M,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\sigma)}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}})\in{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}

which follows directly from the definition of
{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\varphi)
and from the
inductive hypothesis applied to
(n,\varphi,\mathsf{v})
.

Assume last that
\sigma={\varphi}\multimap{\tau}
and that

(M,u)\in{\mathcal{R}({\varphi}\multimap{\tau})}^{\mathord{-}}
, we must prove that

(M,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi}\multimap{\tau})}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi\Rightarrow\tau)}^{\mathord{+}}
. Let
(V,v)\in{\mathcal{V}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{-}}
,
we must prove that

\displaystyle(\langle M\rangle V,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi}\multimap{\tau})}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}}\,v)\in{\mathcal{R}(\tau)}^{\mathord{+}}

(118)

which follows from the fact that

{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,{\varphi}\multimap{\tau})}\right]{}}\,{u^{!}}\,v=\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{g}}(n,\tau)}\right]{(u({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v}))^{!}}
.
Indeed the inductive hypothesis applied to
(n,\varphi)
yields

(V,{\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v})\in{\mathcal{R}(\varphi)}^{\mathord{+}}
and hence

(\langle M\rangle V,{u}\,{({\left[{{\mathsf{p}}^{\mathsf{v}}(n,\varphi)}\right]{}}\,{v})})\in{\mathcal{R}(\tau)}^{\mathord{+}}
by
Lemma
22
, from which we derive (
118
)
by Lemma
34
and Lemma
30
.
∎

Lemma 36
.

For any closed type
\sigma
one has

{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}={\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}
.

Proof.

Immediate consequence of lemmas
30
,

34
and
35
.
∎

From now on we simply use
the notation
\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
instead of
{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{-}}
and

{\mathcal{R}(\sigma)}^{\mathord{+}}
.

4.5.
Logical relation lemma

We can prove now the main result of this section.

Theorem 37

(Logical Relation Lemma)
.

Assume that
x_{1}:\varphi_{1},\dots,x_{k}:\varphi_{k}\vdash M:\sigma
and let

(where

is a value and

. Then
({M}\left[{V_{1}/x_{1},\dots,V_{k}/x_{k}}\right],\left[{M}\right]^{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}}{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern Remark
.

One would expect to have rather assumptions of the shape
“
(V_{i},v_{i})\in\mathcal{V}(\varphi_{i})
”; the problem is that we don’t know whether

\mathcal{V}(\varphi_{i})^{\mathord{+}}=\mathcal{V}(\varphi_{i})^{\mathord{-}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation of
M
, that is, on
M
. We set

\mathcal{P}=(x_{1}:\varphi_{1},\dots,x_{k}:\varphi_{k})
and, given a term
R
, we use
R^{\prime}
for
the term
{R}\left[{V_{1}/x_{1},\dots,V_{k}/x_{k}}\right]
. We also use

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle v\hfil

for the
sequence
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}
and

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle x\hfil

for the sequence
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}
.

The case
M=x_{i}
is straightforward.

15

. We have

and hence

as contended.

Assume that

and

\sigma={\psi_{\ell}}\otimes{\psi_{r}}
with

for

i\in\{\ell,r\}
. By inductive hypothesis we have

(N^{\prime}_{i},{\left[{N_{i}}\right]^{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern The case
M=\mathsf{in}_{i}N
(and
\sigma={\psi_{\ell}}\oplus{\psi_{r}}
) is handled
similarly, using Lemma
25
.

Assume that
M=\mathsf{fold}(N)
and
\sigma=\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi
with

\mathcal{P}\vdash N:{\psi}\left[{\varphi}/{\zeta}\right]
. By inductive hypothesis we have

, we prove that

\displaystyle\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}}\quad(M^{\prime},f^{k}(0))\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma)\,.

(119)

The base case is proven by Lemma
30
. Assume that

(M^{\prime},f^{k}(0))\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
. Choosing an arbitrary
\varepsilon
, we have

((M^{\prime})^{\mathord{\oc}},(f^{k}(0))^{!})\in\mathcal{V}(\oc\sigma)^{\varepsilon}\subseteq\mathcal{R}(\oc\sigma)
and
hence by our “outermost” inductive hypothesis we have

({N^{\prime}}\left[{(M^{\prime})^{\mathord{\oc}}}/{x}\right],f^{k+1}(0))\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
from which we get

(M^{\prime},f^{k+1}(0))\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
by Lemma
21

and this ends the proof of (
119
). We conclude that

that is

0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle v\hfil$\crcr}}}))\in\mathcal{R}(\sigma)
, by
Lemma
21
.

Assume that
M=\mathsf{unfold}(N)
where
\mathcal{P}\vdash N:\varphi
with

\varphi=\textsf{Rec}\,\zeta\cdot\psi
. We apply Lemma
28

straightforwardly.

Assume that
M=()
and the typing derivation consists of the axiom

\mathcal{P}\vdash():\mathbf{1}
so that
\sigma=\mathbf{1}
. We have
(M,\left[{M}\right]{})\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})
by definition since

\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{M,()}=1=\left[{M}\right]{}
.

Assume last that
M=\mathsf{coin}(p)
for some
p\in[0,1]\cap\mathbb{Q}
and the typing
derivation consists of the axiom
\mathcal{P}\vdash\mathsf{coin}(p):{\mathbf{1}}\oplus{\mathbf{1}}
so
that
\sigma={\mathbf{1}}\oplus{\mathbf{1}}
. We must prove that
(\mathsf{coin}(p),\left[{\mathsf{coin}(p)}\right]{})\in\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{1}}\oplus{\mathbf{1}})
. Remember that
\left[{\mathsf{coin}(p)}\right]{}=pe_{(\ell,*)}+(1-p)e_{(r,*)}
. Let
\varepsilon\in\{\mathord{-},\mathord{+}\}
and
let
(T,t)\in({\mathcal{V}({\mathbf{1}}\oplus{\mathbf{1}})}\multimap{{\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})}})^{\varepsilon}
, we must
prove that
(\langle T\rangle\mathsf{coin}(p),t(pe_{(\ell,*)}+(1-p)e_{(r,*)}))\in\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{1})
. We have

\displaystyle\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle T\rangle\mathsf{coin}(p),()}=p\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle T\rangle\mathsf{in}_{\ell}(),()}+(1-p)\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle T\rangle\mathsf{in}_{r}(),()}

since the first reduction step must be
\mathsf{coin}(p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathsf{in}_{\ell}()
or

\mathsf{coin}(p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1-p}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathsf{in}_{r}()
. By our assumption on
(T,t)
we have

\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle T\rangle\mathsf{in}_{i}()}\geq t(e_{(i,*)})
and hence

\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle T\rangle\mathsf{coin}(p),()}\geq t(pe_{(\ell,*)}+(1-p)e_{(r,*)})
as contended, by
linearity of
t
.
∎

Theorem 38

(Adequacy)
.

Let
M
be a closed term such that
\vdash M:\mathbf{1}
. Then
\left[{M}\right]{}=\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{M,()}
.

By Corollary
18
and Theorem
37
.

5.
Full Abstraction

We prove now the Full Abstraction Theorem
41
, that is the converse of the Adequacy Theorem.

5.1.
Outline of the proof.

We reason by contrapositive. Assume that two closed terms
M_{1}
and
M_{2}

have different semantics. Remember from Section
3.2
, that a
closed term of type
\sigma
is interpreted as a vector with indices in the web

|{\left[{\sigma}\right]}|
, so that there is
a\in|{\left[{\sigma}\right]}|
such that

\left[{M_{1}}\right]_{a}\neq\left[{M_{2}}\right]_{a}
. We want to design a term that will separate

M_{1}
and
M_{2}
observationally.

We define a
testing term

\vdash a^{-}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{({\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}})}

that will depend only on the structure of the element
a
of the
web. We then use properties of the semantics (namely that terms of
type
{\oc\iota}\multimap{\tau}
can be seen as power series) to find reals
\vec{p}
such
that
\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}
separates
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
:

\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{1},()}\neq\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{2},()}

Let us detail the key points of the proof.

Remember from Section
3.2.7
that, because

\vdash a^{-}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{({\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}})}
, its
interpretation
\left[{a^{-}}\right]
can be seen as a power series

\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}(\vec{\zeta})=\left[{a^{-}}\right]\,{\vec{\zeta}\ ^{!}}=\left(\sum_{[k_{1},\dots,k_{n}]\in|{\oc{\iota}}|}\left[{a^{-}}\right]_{[k_{1},\dots,k_{n}],b}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{k_{i}}\right)_{b\in|{{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}|}

with infinitely many parameters

\vec{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\dots,\zeta_{n},\dots)
. Moreover, if

\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\zeta_{i}\leq 1
, then
\vec{\zeta}\in\mathsf{P}{\left[{\iota}\right]}
and

\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}(\vec{\zeta})\in\mathsf{P}{\left[{{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}\right]}
(see
Theorem
6
).

The first key point is to remark that the testing term
a^{-}
is defined in
such a way that
{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}
has actually only finitely many parameters

\zeta_{0},\dots,\zeta_{|a|^{-}}
(meaning that if the support of the multiset
c
is not
included in
\{0,\dots,|a|^{-}\}
, then
\left[{a^{-}}\right]_{(c,b)}=0
). Now, for any
u\in\mathsf{P}{\left[{\sigma}\right]}
,
\left[{\lambda y^{\oc\sigma}\,\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle y}\right]u^{!}\in\mathsf{P}{\left[{{\oc\iota}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}\right]}
. It is also a power series that depends on the same finitely many parameters
\zeta_{0},\dots,\zeta_{|a|^{-}}
.

The second key point is a separation property of
\left[{a^{-}}\right]
:
we prove that, in the power series

\left[{\lambda y^{\oc\sigma}\,\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle y}\right]u^{!}
,
the coefficient of the unitary monomial
16
16

16

That is, the monomial
where each exponent is equal to one.

\prod_{k=0}^{|a|^{-}}\zeta_{k}

is equal to
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})\,u_{a}
with a coefficient
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})\neq 0

which depends only on
a
. Now, by assumption,
\left[{M_{1}}\right]_{a}
and

\left[{M_{2}}\right]_{a}
have different coefficient.
For
i=1,2
, we have

{\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{i}}\right]}=\left[{\lambda y^{\oc\sigma}\,\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle y}\right]{\left[{M_{i}}\right]^{!}}
.
Thus, the power series

{\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{i}}\right]}

(for
i=1,2
) have
different coefficients
.

The last key point uses classical analysis: if two power series with
non-negative real coefficients and finitely many parameters have different
coefficients, then they differ on non-negative arguments
\vec{p}
close enough
to zero:
\sum p_{i}\leq 1
, so that
\vec{p}\in\mathsf{P}{\left[{\iota}\right]}
and

{\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{1}}\right]}\vec{p}\,^{!}\neq{\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{2}}\right]}\vec{p}\,^{!}
.

Finally, in order to substitute in
a^{-}
the parameters
\vec{\zeta}
with
the reals
\vec{p}
, we use
\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})
as introduced in
Paragraph
2.3
. Indeed,

\left[{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{i}}\right]=\widehat{\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{i}}\right]}\vec{p}\,^{!}
.
We conclude thanks to the Adequacy Theorem
38
that ensures
that

\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{1},()}\neq\mathsf{Red}^{\infty}_{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\vec{p})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M^{\mathord{\oc}}_{2},()}

5.2.
Notations.

In order to define the testing term
a^{-}
, we will reason by
induction and we will need to associate three kinds of
testing
terms
with the points of the webs. More precisely:

•

Given a positive type
\varphi
and
a\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
, we define a term

a^{0}
such that

\displaystyle\vdash a^{0}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{{\varphi}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}.

•

Given a general type
\sigma
and
a\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
, we define
terms
a^{+}
and

a^{-}
such that

\displaystyle\vdash a^{+}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{\sigma}

\displaystyle\vdash a^{-}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}.

We also introduce natural numbers
|a|^{0}
,
|a|^{-}
and
|a|^{+}

depending only on
a
. They represent the finite numbers of parameters
on which the power series
\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}
,

\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}
and
\widehat{\left[{a^{+}}\right]}
depend
respectively.

We denote as
\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})
,
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})
and
\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})
natural numbers
depending only on
a
and that will appear as the coefficient of the
unitary monomial
\prod_{k=0}^{|a|^{0}}\zeta_{k}
,

\prod_{k=0}^{|a|^{-}}\zeta_{k}
and
\prod_{k=0}^{|a|^{+}}\zeta_{k}

respectively of the corresponding power series. These numbers are all

>0
.

We use the terms introduced in the probabilistic tests paragraph of Subsection
2.3
and whose semantics are given in Subsection
3.3
:

•

\mathsf{ran}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}})
which reduces to
\underline{i}
with probability
p_{i}
for

\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}p_{i}\leq 1
,

•

M_{0}\cdot N
which reduces to
V
with probability
p\,q
if
M_{0}

reduces to
()
with probability
p
and
N
reduces to
V
with
probability
q
,

•

M_{0}\and\dots\and M_{k-1}
which reduces to
()
with probability

\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}p_{i}
if
M_{i}
reduces to
()
with probability
p_{i}
,

•

\langle\mathsf{choose}_{k}^{\sigma}(M_{0},\dots,M_{k-1})\rangle P
which reduces to
M_{i}

with probability
p_{i}
if
p_{i}
is the probability
of
P
to reduce to
\underline{i}
,

•

\langle\mathsf{ext}\left(l,\,r\right)\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z
and
\langle\mathsf{win}_{k}({\vec{n}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z
to
partition the parameters
\mathsf{der}\,Z
. Indeed
Z
will denote a variable
of type
\oc\iota
and
\mathsf{der}\,Z
has to be considered as the
sequence of parameters

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil

of the power series
interpreting testing terms.
|a|^{0}
,
|a|^{-}
and
|a|^{+}

represent the number of parameters on which the respective testing
terms depend. We use
\langle\mathsf{win}_{i}({\vec{n}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z
to extract
subsequences of

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil

that will be given as arguments to
subterms in the inductive definition of the testing terms. Remember
that
\langle\mathsf{win}_{i}({n_{0},\dots,n_{k}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{l}}}{{\rightarrow}}\underline{l}
if

l
is in the
i
th window of size
n_{i}
, that is

n_{0}+\cdots+n_{i-1}\leq l\leq n_{0}+\cdots+n_{i}-1
and
p_{l}
is the
probability that
\mathsf{der}\,Z
reduces to
\underline{l}
, that is the
non-negative real parameter
\zeta_{l}
.
This is a key ingredient in the computation of the coefficient of the
unitary monomial of the interpretation of testing terms by induction
on type and on the structure of
a
.

5.3.
Testing terms.

We define the terms
a^{0}
,
a^{+}
and
a^{-}
and
the associated natural numbers
|a|^{0}
,
|a|^{+}
and
|a|^{-}
, by induction
on the structure of the point
a
.

Let
\varphi
be a positive type and
a\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
. We define

a^{0}
and
a^{+}
by induction on the size of
a
using the structure
of
\varphi

Let
\varphi=\oc\tau
and
a=[{b_{0},\dots,b_{k-1}}]
with

b_{i}\in|{[{\tau}]}|
.

By inductive hypothesis, we have built terms

\vdash b_{i}^{-}:{\oc\iota}\multimap{{\oc\tau}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}
and
\vdash b_{i}^{+}:{{\oc\iota}}\multimap{\tau}
. Then
we set

a^{0}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda x^{\oc\tau}\,\langle\langle b_{0}^{-}\rangle{\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{0}({|b_{0}|^{-},\dots,|b_{k-1}|^{-}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)}^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle x\and\cdots\\
\and\langle\langle b_{k-1}^{-}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{k-1}({|b_{0}|^{-},\dots,|b_{k-1}|^{-}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle x,\\
\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{m}^{-}({b_{i}}),\ \text{ and }|a|^{0}=|b_{0}|^{-}+\cdots+|b_{k-1}|^{-}\,.

a^{+}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\left(\langle\mathsf{choose}^{\tau}_{k}\left(\langle b_{0}^{+}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{1}({k,|b_{0}|^{+},\cdots,|b_{k-1}|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\right.\right.,\dots,\\
\left.\left.\langle b_{k-1}^{+}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{k}({k,|b_{0}|^{+},\cdots,|b_{k-1}|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\right)\rangle{\mathsf{der}\,Z}\right)^{\mathord{\oc}},\\
\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})={a}!\,\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{m}^{+}({b_{i}}),\ \text{ and }|a|^{+}=k+|b_{0}|^{+}+\cdots+|b_{k-1}|^{+}\,.

Remember that the factorial
{a}!
of a multiset
a
has been defined in
Paragraph
3.2.7
as the number of permutations that fix
a
.

If
\varphi={\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}}
and
a=(b_{\ell},b_{r})
with

b_{i}\in|{[{\varphi_{i}}]}|
for
i\in\{\ell,r\}
,

then we set

a^{0}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda x^{\varphi}\,\langle\langle b_{\ell}^{0}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{0}({|b_{\ell}|^{0},|b_{r}|^{0}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle\mathsf{pr}_{\ell}x\and\langle\langle b_{r}^{0}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{1}({|b_{\ell}|^{0},|b_{r}|^{0}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle\mathsf{pr}_{r}x,\\
\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{0}({b_{\ell}})\,\mathbb{m}^{0}({b_{r}}),\ \text{ and }|a|^{0}=|b_{\ell}|^{0}+|b_{r}|^{0}\,.

a^{+}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\left(\langle b_{\ell}^{+}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{0}({|b_{\ell}|^{+},|b_{r}|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}},\langle b_{r}^{+}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{1}({|b_{\ell}|^{+},|b_{r}|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\right),\\
\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{+}({b_{\ell}})\,\mathbb{m}^{+}({b_{r}})\text{ and }|a|^{+}=|b_{\ell}|^{+}+|b_{r}|^{+}\,.

If
\varphi={\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}}
and
a=(\ell,a_{\ell})
with
b\in|{[{\varphi_{\ell}}]}|

(the case
a=(r,a_{r})
is similar),

then we set

\displaystyle a^{0}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda x^{{\varphi_{\ell}}\oplus{\varphi_{r}}}\,\mathsf{case}(x,y_{\ell}\cdot\langle\langle a_{\ell}^{0}\rangle Z\rangle y_{\ell},y_{r}\cdot\Omega^{\mathbf{1}}),\quad\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{0}({a_{\ell}})\quad\text{and }|a|^{0}=|a_{\ell}|^{0}\,.

\displaystyle a^{+}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\mathsf{in}_{\ell}\langle a^{+}_{\ell}\rangle Z,\quad\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{+}({a_{\ell}})\quad\text{and }|a|^{+}=|a_{\ell}|^{+}\,.

Finally, for a general type
\sigma
and
a\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
, we define
a^{+}
and
a^{-}
.

If
\sigma=\varphi
is positive,

then we have already defined
a^{+}
.

Let us now define
a^{-}
. This term does not
depend on the structure of
\varphi
:

\displaystyle a^{-}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda x^{\oc\varphi}\,\langle\langle a^{0}\rangle Z\rangle\mathsf{der}\,x,\quad\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})\quad\text{and }|a|^{-}=|a|^{0}\,.

If
\sigma={\varphi}\multimap{\tau}
and
a=(b,c)

with
b\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
and
c\in|{[{\tau}]}|
,

then we set

a^{+}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda x^{\varphi}\,{{\langle\langle b^{0}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{0}({|b|^{0},|c|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle x}\,\cdot\,{{\langle c^{+}\rangle\left({\langle\mathsf{win}_{1}({|b|^{0},|c|^{+}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z}\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}}}}\,,\\
\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{0}({b})\,\mathbb{m}^{+}({c})\,,\text{ and }|a|^{+}=|b|^{0}+|c|^{+}\,.

a^{-}=\lambda Z^{\oc\iota}\,\lambda f^{\oc({\varphi}\multimap{\tau})}\,{\langle\langle c^{-}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{1}({|b|^{+},|c|^{-}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{der}\,f\rangle\langle b^{+}\rangle\left(\langle\mathsf{win}_{0}({|b|^{+},|c|^{-}})\rangle\mathsf{der}\,Z\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}\right)^{\mathord{\oc}}}\\
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})=\mathbb{m}^{+}({b})\,\mathbb{m}^{-}({c}),\,\text{ and }|a|^{-}=|b|^{+}+|c|^{-}\,.

It is easy to check that these terms satisfy the announced typing judgments. It
is also clear that
\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})
,
\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})
and
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})
are non zero natural numbers.

We will now tackle the proof of the main observation: that is
that
the semantics of

a^{-}
is a power series with finitely many parameters
and whose coefficient of the unitary monomial can be seen as a
morphism in
\mathsf{P}{[{{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}
.

Lemma
39

introduces notations for the unitary monomials and provides
useful properties for proving the key
Lemma
40
which gives the coefficients of these
monomials.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\sigma
be a general type and

t\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\oc\iota}\multimap{\sigma}}]}
.

(1)

Assume that there is
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for any
c\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
, the power series
\widehat{t}_{c}

over
\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
depends on the
k
first parameters.
For any
c\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
, let us denote as
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)_{c}
the coefficient of the monomial
\zeta_{0}\dots\zeta_{k-1}
of
\widehat{t}_{c}
. Then,
k^{-k}\,\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
.

(2)

Assume moreover that
\sigma={\varphi}\multimap{\tau}
where
\varphi
is
a positive type and
\tau

a general type.
Let
m\in\mathsf{P}{[{\tau}]}

and
a\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
.
If
\forall u\in\mathsf{P}^{\mathord{\oc}}{[{\varphi}]^{\oc}}\ \mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)u=mu_{a}
then
\forall u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}\ \mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)u=mu_{a}
.

Proof.

We prove (
1
).

First notice that
\forall c\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
,
the coefficient of the monomial
\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\zeta_{i}
is
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)_{c}=t_{([0,\dots,k-1],c)}
. Now, let

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle\tfrac{1}{k}\hfil

be the sequence of
k
coefficients all equal to
\frac{1}{k}
:

\widehat{t}_{c}(\vec{\tfrac{1}{k}})=\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in|{[{\oc\iota}]}|\\
\operatorname{\mathsf{supp}}({\mu})\subseteq\{0,\dots,k-1\}\end{subarray}}t_{(\mu,c)}{\tfrac{1}{k}}^{\#{\mu}}

so that
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)_{c}\,k^{-k}\leq\widehat{t}(\vec{\tfrac{1}{k}})_{c}
. Since
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{k}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\tfrac{1}{k}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\tfrac{1}{k}\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\tfrac{1}{k}\hfil$\crcr}}}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
,
\widehat{t}(\vec{\tfrac{1}{k}})\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
which is downward closed, we have that

k^{-k}\,\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
.

Now we prove (
2
).

For any
a\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
, there is
A
such that
u_{a}\leq A

for any
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
(see
Subsection
3.2
). Hence, for any

u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
,
\tfrac{u_{a}}{A}m\in\mathsf{P}{[{\tau}]}
and we
deduce thanks to Lemma
3
that

u\mapsto u_{a}\,m\,A^{-1}
is in

\mathsf{P}{[{{\varphi}\multimap{\tau}}]}
. Without loss of generality, we can
choose
A\geq k^{k}
so that
u\mapsto u_{a}\,m\,A^{-1}
and

u\mapsto A^{-1}\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)u
are both in

\mathsf{P}{[{{\varphi}\multimap{\tau}}]}
. Now, since
[{\varphi}]^{\oc}

is dense (see
Theorem
16
), if

u\mapsto A^{-1}\,\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)u
and

u\mapsto A^{-1}\,u_{a}\,m
are equal on all coalgebraic points

u\in\mathsf{P}^{\mathord{\oc}}{[{\varphi}]^{\oc}}
, they are equal (see
Definition
12
). Thus, for all
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
,

u_{a}\,m=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{t}\right)u
.
∎

We are now ready to prove that the coefficient of the
unitary monomial of a testing term associated with a point
a
of the
web allows to extract the
a
-coefficient of an argument, up to a
non-zero coefficient depending only on
a
.

This is central in the proof of Full
Abstraction.
Let us first introduce some notations that will be used along this proof.

Intuitively, for
s\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\oc\iota}\multimap{\sigma}}]}
, we
reason on power series
\widehat{s}
with values in

\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
. But formally, we reason on the non-negative
real power series
\widehat{s}_{a^{\prime}}
defined for each

a^{\prime}\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
and for all
parameters
17
17

17

We follow the common
mathematical practice of using the same notation

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern to refer to the formal parameters of a
power series and to real arguments of the corresponding
function.

\vec{\zeta}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
as
\widehat{s}_{a^{\prime}}(\vec{\zeta})=(\widehat{s}(\vec{\zeta}))_{a^{\prime}}=(s\left.\vec{\zeta}\,\right.^{!})_{a^{\prime}}
(see
Paragraph
3.2.7
).

We want to compute the unitary monomial of
\widehat{s}
which will be in
\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
. We define it for each
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
as
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(s\right)_{a^{\prime}}=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{s}_{a^{\prime}}\right)
.

We will also use the fact that a morphism

t\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\varphi}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}
is defined by the collection of

t_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}

for
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
and is extensionally characterized
by its values
t\,u
on every
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
. Indeed, for
any
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
, there is
\varepsilon>0
such that

\varepsilon e_{a^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
and

t_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}\,(t\,\varepsilon e_{a^{\prime}})_{\ast}
by
linearity of matrix multiplication.

Lemma 40
.

Let
\sigma
be a type and
a\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
.

(1)

Assume that
\sigma=\varphi
is positive. If
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
, then

\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}
is a power series over

\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
depending on
|a|^{0}
parameters, so we define
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}\right)
and check that

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\varphi}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}
and that
for any
u\in\mathsf{P}{([{\varphi}])}
,

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)u=\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})\,u_{a}
.

(2)

Assume that
\sigma
is a general type. For any
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
,
\widehat{\left[{a^{+}}\right]}_{a^{\prime}}

is a power series over
\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
depending on
|a|^{+}
parameters, so we define
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{+}}\right]\right)_{a^{\prime}}=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{\left[{a^{+}}\right]}_{a^{\prime}}\right)
and check that
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{+}}\right]\right)\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
and that

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{+}}\right]\right)=\mathbb{m}^{+}({a})\,e_{a}
where
e_{a}
is the base vector such that
(e_{a})_{a^{\prime}}=\delta_{{a^{\prime}},{a}}
for
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
.

(3)

Let
\sigma
be a general type.
For any
a^{\prime}\in|{[{\oc\sigma}]}|
,
\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}
is a power series over
\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
depending on
|a|^{-}
parameters, so we define
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{-}}\right]\right)_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}\right)
and check that

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{-}}\right]\right)\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\oc\sigma}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}

and that for any
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\sigma}]}
,

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{-}}\right]\right)u^{!}=\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})\,u_{a}
.

Proof.

Let us argue by mutual induction on the size of
a
and the structure of
\varphi
.

Let
\varphi
be a positive type and
a\in|{[{\varphi}]}|
. We prove (
2
) and (
1
) by induction on the structure
of
\varphi

Assume that
\varphi=\oc\tau
and that
a=[{b_{0},\dots,b_{k-1}}]

with

b_{i}\in|{[{\tau}]}|
.

We prove (
1
). Let

a^{\prime}=[{b^{\prime}_{0},\dots,b^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}-1}}]\in[{\oc{\tau}}]
with

b^{\prime}_{j}\in|{[{\tau}]}|
and
\vec{\zeta}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
be the
concatenation of the finite sequences
18
18

18

We assume
that the support of indices of the sequences are disjoint even if
this requires some renaming.

\vec{\zeta}^{i}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}
such
that the length of
\vec{\zeta}^{i}
is
|b_{i}|^{-}
.

By Theorem
16
,

\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}(\vec{\zeta})=(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\,\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!})_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}\in\mathsf{P}{[{{\oc\tau}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}

is completely determined by the function
u\mapsto\left[{a^{0}}\right]\,\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}\,u^{!}
defined on
\mathsf{P}{\left[{\tau}\right]}
.
By inductive hypothesis,

\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}
depends on finitely many
parameters
|a|^{0}=|b_{0}|^{-}+\dots+|b_{k-1}|^{-}
, since

\displaystyle\left[{a^{0}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}u^{!}=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\left[{b_{i}^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{i}\right.^{!}u^{!}\qquad\text{and therefore}\qquad\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)u^{!}=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}^{i}}\left(\left[{b_{i}^{-}}\right]\right)u^{!}.

Again, by inductive hypothesis it follows that

\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)u^{!}=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbb{m}^{-}({b_{i}})\,u_{b_{i}}=\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})\,(u^{!})_{a}.

We can apply (
2
) of Lemma
39
, so that we have
\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)u=\mathbb{m}^{0}({a})\,u_{a}
for all
u\in\mathsf{P}{[{\oc\tau}]}
.

).
Let

and

Assume that

By Theorem

\widehat{\left[{a^{0}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}(\vec{\zeta})=(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\,\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!})_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}\in\mathsf{P}{[{{{\varphi_{\ell}}\otimes{\varphi_{r}}}\multimap{\mathbf{1}}}]}

Besides,

\displaystyle=\left[{b_{\ell}^{0}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{\ell}\right.^{!}u_{\ell}\,\left[{b_{r}^{0}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{r}\right.^{!}u_{r}\quad\text{ and therefore }\quad\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{0}}\right]\right)u=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}^{\ell}}\left(\left[{b_{\ell}^{0}}\right]\right)u_{\ell}\,\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}^{r}}\left(\left[{b_{r}^{0}}\right]\right)u_{r}.

\displaystyle\widehat{\left[{a^{-}}\right]}_{(a^{\prime},\ast)}(\vec{\zeta})=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\left[{a^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}\varepsilon e_{a^{\prime}})_{\ast}=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}(\left[{c^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{2}\right.^{!}\left(\mathsf{der}_{[{{\varphi}\multimap{\tau}}]}(\varepsilon e_{a^{\prime}})\left[{b^{+}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{1}\right.^{!}\right)^{!})_{\ast}.

Let now

and we compute:

\displaystyle\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}}\left(\left[{a^{-}}\right]\right){u^{!}}=\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}^{1}}\left(\mathbb{c}^{\mathbb{1}}_{\vec{\zeta}^{2}}\left(\left[{c^{-}}\right]\right)\left({u\left[{b^{+}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}^{1}\right.^{!}}\right)^{!}\right)\,.

\varepsilon>0
such that

\varepsilon e_{b}\in\mathsf{P}{[{\varphi}]}
), so that we can apply

to

. Now, by using inductive hypothesis, we get

Theorem 41

(Full Abstraction)
.

If
\vdash M_{1}:\sigma
and
\vdash M_{2}:\sigma
satisfy
M_{1}\mathrel{\sim}M_{2}
then

\left[{M_{1}}\right]=\left[{M_{2}}\right]
.

Proof.

By contrapositive. Assume that

\left[{M_{1}}\right]\not=\left[{M_{2}}\right]
. There is
a\in|{[{\sigma}]}|
such
that
\left[{M_{1}}\right]_{a}\neq\left[{M_{2}}\right]_{a}
. Then by
Lemma
40
,

\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M_{i}^{\mathord{\oc}}}\right]
,
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, are power series with different coefficients,
namely the coefficients of the monomial

\zeta_{0}\dots\zeta_{|a|^{-}-1}
are
\mathbb{m}^{-}({a})\,\left[{M_{i}}\right]_{a}
for

i\in\{1,2\}
as

\left[{\lambda x^{\oc\iota}\,\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle x\rangle M_{i}^{\mathord{\oc}}}\right]{\left.\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}\right.^{!}}=\left[{a^{-}}\right]{\left.\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}\right.^{!}}{\left[{M_{i}}\right]^{!}}
.
There is
\vec{\zeta}=(\zeta_{0},\dots,\zeta_{|a|^{-}-1})\in\mathsf{P}{[{\iota}]}

with
\zeta_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}\cap[0,1]
such that

\left[{a^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}\left[{M_{1}}\right]^{!}\neq\left[{a^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}\left[{M_{2}}\right]^{!}
.
Yet,

\left[{\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M_{i}^{\mathord{\oc}}}\right]=\left[{a^{-}}\right]\left.\vec{\zeta}\right.^{!}({\left[{M_{i}}\right]})
.
By Theorem
38
, we get that

\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M_{1}^{\mathord{\oc}}
and

\langle\langle a^{-}\rangle\mathsf{ran}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle\zeta\hfil$\crcr}}})^{\mathord{\oc}}\rangle M_{2}^{\mathord{\oc}}

converge to
()
with different probabilities. It follows that

M_{1}\mathrel{\not\sim}M_{2}
.
∎
